# General LLM Inference Acceleration — Gap Map (CLOSED / OPEN / ABANDONED / HARDWARE-RULED-OUT / NEVER-DISCUSSED)

**Compiled:** 2026-09-14 · **Window:** prioritised 2024-06 → 2026-09; older work only where it is the canonical reference for a still-live question.
**Target setup (this constraint decided every relevance judgement):** ONE GPU — **RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell, 96 GB, sm120** (compute capability 12.0), driver 580, 208 CPU cores, single node, **no NVLink / no InfiniBand / no cluster**. Software: **vLLM 0.29.0**, **SGLang 0.5.19**. Target model **Qwen3-4B** (dense, full attention — not MoE, not hybrid Mamba, not MLA). Drafters on the rig: dflash2 (DFlash/MTP-class) and an EAGLE3 Qwen3-4B drafter.

**Scope.** This map owns the acceleration axes that the two sibling maps in this repo do **not**: attention kernels/backends and backend selection on sm120; compilation & CUDA-graph modes; **weight/activation** quantization; batching/scheduling and host-bound or CPU-side decode; sampling, logits, structured-output/grammar and tokenizer/detokenizer overhead; long-context *attention computation* (not KV policy); serving-layer/API overhead; model-side training-free rewrites; and measurement methodology for acceleration claims.
**Deliberately NOT re-mapped** (already owned, per `spec-decode-gap-map-2026-09-13.md` and `KV_CACHE_GAP_MAP.md`): KV-cache management in every form (paging, prefix caching, eviction, KV quantization, offload/tiering, disaggregated P/D) and speculative decoding (drafters, draft training, n-gram drafting, verification, acceptance length). Where a row's *subject* is a CUDA-graph mode or an attention backend that merely happens to be exercised by the dflash drafter, it is in scope; the drafting algorithm is not.

## How this map was built, and how much to trust each row

* **Fan-out.** 19 parallel researchers each swept one subtopic: 14 on the acceleration axes (S1–S14) and 5 on the never-discussed test (E1–E5). They harvested **730 raw evidence blocks** across five sections. Two files were produced by named sub-researchers and are folded in as supporting material (`S9_sub_runtimes.md`, `S9_sub_structural.md`, `S10_gh_sub.md`).
* **Adversarial verification layer.** Every block was then re-checked by a **separate verifier that had not written it** (V1–V4, reports on disk as `S*.verify.md` / `E*.verify.md`). Verifiers re-fetched every URL over HTTP, re-located every claimed verbatim quote, re-read every artifact's own state token, read back every arXiv ID, and actively tried to **falsify** each status claim. Totals: **S1 25/1/0, S2 33/5/0, S3 38/5/0, S4 26/2/0, S5 28/6/0, S6 28/3/1, S7 37/2/0, S8 28/3/0, S9 41/5/0, S10 76/10/0, S13 83/8/0, S14 61/19/0** (checked/corrected/dropped) and **E1–E5: 87 rows checked, 87/87 gap quotes re-located, 23 absence claims falsified, 24 rows removed**. 17 verification reports exist; none reported "could not verify".
* **Row counts after de-duplication and after applying the verifiers' corrections: 156 CLOSED, 43 OPEN, 312 ATTEMPTED-AND-ABANDONED, 130 HARDWARE-RULED-OUT, 63 NEVER-DISCUSSED — 704 rows.** The OPEN section is **43, slightly above the 30–40 target**; the four extra are rows that verification *moved into* OPEN from ABANDONED (the artifacts turned out to be still open). They were kept rather than silently deleted.
* **Classification corrections forced by verification are already applied** and are listed individually in the correction log at the end of this file. The most consequential: **a row claiming "merged (both)" where one PR was closed-unmerged** (S9 A9.4); **six ABANDONED rows whose artifacts are actually still open**, moved to Section B (S3 C2/C3/C7, S4 C.4.5, plus two re-framed); **one row marked OPEN that verification showed was already closed** (S14 B1.2); **one Section-A row whose "merged" status was false** (TGI #138, re-pointed at #202 and moved to Section C); **23 Section-E absence claims falsified by finding an actual human thread**; and **one genuinely fabricated quotation** (S2 C.2), which was dropped outright.
* **Hardware-quote integrity.** The brief for this map specifically warned about a hardware quote that had been **silently edited inside the quotation marks** (deleting "with four GPUs"). All **83 S13 hardware quotes were re-fetched individually and mechanised**: **82/83 relocate verbatim at the row's own cited URL**, and **all GPU-count words were verified present in every count-bearing quote** ("up to 8 GPUs", "four A100 GPUs", "256 GPUs", "four H100 80GB", "four A100 nodes, each with 8 80GB", "8xH100", "8×H100", "DP=8", "one H100"). **The one exception (D.3.3) is a provenance defect, not an edit: the quote is verbatim in the DeepEP README its NOTE names but not on its cited PR page — zero words altered, zero deleted.** Separately, two D-section rows were found to have **opposite-polarity quotations** (the quote asserted the reverse of its bucket) and one **D.5 row lacked the required single-GPU sentence**; those are corrected in place and their unrepaired residue is stated in the row.
* **Evidence labels are per item.** `READ BODY` = the page/PDF/source/JSON payload was actually retrieved and read. `TITLE ONLY`, `TITLE + STATE ONLY` and `APPROXIMATE` are used where only a title or an altered quote was available; **TITLE ONLY is never the sole basis for a claim.** Composite quotes carry an explicit `(composite: quoted fragments interleaved with retrieved framing text)` label rather than being presented as one continuous quotation.
* **Measured fidelity of Section C's quotation fields.** A **blind 40-item random sample** of Section C's reason fields (seed 20260914) was re-fetched at source and matched mechanically: **33/40 = 82% reproduce verbatim as one continuous quotation at the cited URL**; 3/40 differ only by HTML/JSON escape rendering (ratio ≥0.91, e.g. `\"` vs `"`); 4/40 contain a genuine quotation embedded in retrieved framing text rather than one continuous run. Across the whole corrected corpus the mechanical rate is **559/704 = 79%**, and **Section C specifically is 280/312 = 90%**.
* **Measured fidelity of Section C's quotation fields.** A **blind 40-item random sample** of Section C's reason fields was re-fetched at source and matched mechanically. Two independent samples at different seeds: **34/40 = 85%** (seed 20260914) and **36/40 = 90%** (seed 777) reproduce verbatim as one continuous quotation at the cited URL — **combined 70/80 = 87.5%**. In the primary sample, 2/40 differ only by HTML/JSON escape rendering (ratio ≥0.91, e.g. `\"` vs `"`) and 4/40 contain a genuine quotation embedded in retrieved framing text rather than one continuous run. Across the whole corrected corpus the mechanical rate is **592/704 = 84%**; per-section: **ABANDONED 91%, HARDWARE-RULED-OUT 89%, OPEN 81%, CLOSED 74%, NEVER-DISCUSSED 67%**.
* **A parser defect found late and fixed — this is why those numbers are as high as they are.** An earlier pass truncated multi-line source quotations at their first line (66 rows affected, including engine code snippets that are only meaningful across lines). The parser was changed to consume continuation lines until the quotation's quotes balance; the measured rate rose from 79% to 84% and every truncated row was eliminated. **Zero quote fields remain truncated mid-quotation.** Read the per-section rates with that history in mind: sections whose rows quote multi-line engine source (NEVER-DISCUSSED, CLOSED) are inherently harder to match than sections whose rows quote a single maintainer sentence (ABANDONED).
* **Residual defects I could not repair, stated plainly.** (1) Of the 704 rows, **88 still fail the strict one-continuous-run test** and 24 more are `NEAR` (a genuine quotation differing only by rendering). Most of the 88 are arXiv HTML renderer artefacts the researchers reproduced deliberately and annotated (`1.84 × \times` for `1.84×`, `[ 23 ] .` for `[23].`, `( B S > 256 BS>256 )` for `(BS > 256)`), a small number are composite fields lacking the mandated label, and the rest are genuine partial matches. (2) **S7 D7.6's quotation is unverified**: the SGLang doc was edited upstream so fragment 1 is no longer at the cited URL and fragments 2–3 live in a different file; the URL was not re-pointed or commit-pinned. (3) **S13 D.2.5's quotation does not establish its bucket** (opposite polarity) though the row's conclusion still holds. (4) **S13 D.1.11 could not be classified** into D.1 or D.5 and is labelled as unclassified rather than deleted. (5) A handful of Section E rows are downgraded rather than removed where the counter-evidence is adjacent but not the same gap.
* **Two defects in the verification tooling were found and worked around, and they matter to how you read the numbers.** `strip_html` treats any `<…>` span as a tag, which silently deletes real source between a stray `<` and a later `>` (a verifier measured ~14 KB lost from vLLM's `CMakeLists.txt`); the checker now also matches against un-stripped raw bytes and against the raw file behind a `blob/` URL. And `fetch.py`'s `STATE-TOKEN` regex prints "merged" for closed-unmerged PRs when the word appears in a timeline cross-reference; every state claim in this map was re-derived from the page's own `state` / `mergedTime` / `stateReason` payload. Where a researcher relied on `STATE-TOKEN` alone, the verifier corrected it.
* **Source discipline.** Primary sources throughout (the actual PR/issue/commit/doc page or the raw source file). Blogs are labelled as blogs. Every arXiv ID was read back at `arxiv.org/abs/<id>` with its title confirmed, because `web_search` has previously returned fabricated arXiv IDs in this project — **one such fabricated-citation catch is recorded**: `2211.15611` was proposed as "SkipDecode" during the sweep and readback returned an unrelated graph-theory paper, so it was dropped and is cited nowhere. The map's own orchestrator also propagated two wrong paper titles from an early draft of the brief (a "Sequoia" long-context paper, which is actually speculative decoding; and a mis-titled Double Sparsity entry); both were caught by researchers and corrected.
* **Not a proposal.** This is an inventory. It contains **no recommendations, no prioritisation, and no "you should build X"**. Another process does the screening.

## Search log (queries actually issued)

**1,656 query lines were logged across the evidence files** and are preserved verbatim in each file's `## SEARCH LOG (queries actually issued)` section. Per-file counts: E1 90 · E2 89 · E3 72 · E4 35 · E5 19 · S0 4 · S1 72 · S10 52 · S10_gh_sub 20 · S11 365 · S12 30 · S13 146 · S14 21 · S2 64 · S3 48 · S4 149 · S5 15 · S6 66 · S7 61 · S8 72 · S9 62 · S9_sub_runtimes 62 · S9_sub_structural 42. Representative routes:

- `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/issues?q=<urlencoded>` — **the only reliably server-rendered GitHub search route in this environment**; honours `is:issue` / `is:pr` / quoted phrases. Used for the unmerged-PR and occupancy sweeps.
- `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/pulls?q=...` — renders a client-side React shell that failed in this environment; not relied on.
- `https://github.com/search?q=...&type=issues` — **secondary-rate-limited to HTTP 429 (or hangs) for most of the session**; several researchers worked around it with the repo-scoped route above and with `web_search` discovery followed by `fetch.py gh <url>` on the individual page.
- `https://arxiv.org/search/?query=...&searchtype=all` — used for paper discovery because `export.arxiv.org/api` returned 429/503 or "Rate exceeded" throughout.
- `https://arxiv.org/abs/<id>` — every arXiv ID read back and title-confirmed.
- `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/<owner>/<repo>/<tag>/<path>` — source-of-truth for every engine flag, default and raise string (vLLM `v0.29.0`, SGLang `v0.5.19`/`main`).
- `docs.vllm.ai`, `docs.sglang.io`, `nvidia.github.io/TensorRT-LLM`, and the in-repo `docs/` trees.

**Environment corrections worth carrying forward** (they cost this sweep real time): `api.github.com` is rate-limited to zero and was not used; `web_fetch` is unreliable here and was not used; bare `curl` to arxiv.org frequently returns HTTP 000 through the local proxy; GitHub **comment bodies ARE present and extractable** from the fetched HTML (`<div class="comment-body">`, and for some pages `<td class="…comment-body…">` or `<clipboard-copy role="menuitem" value="…">`) — a claim that they do not render is false and previously hid real maintainer closure reasons; and `github.com/<repo>/issues?q=` returns `N=0` for a **throttled** page, which is indistinguishable from a genuine zero unless the client is BLOCKED-aware (one confirmed 429 false negative is documented in E3:E10).

## Subsections that genuinely found nothing

These are recorded rather than padded, with the exact queries tried. Each also appears in its file's `## NEGATIVE FINDINGS` section.

- **No maintainer statement anywhere that per-step CPU overhead is a "known unsolved problem"** in vLLM or SGLang. Tried: `is:issue "CPU overhead" scheduler vllm` ; `vllm scheduler bottleneck low batch` ; `"host bound" decode vllm` ; `is:issue "per-step" overhead vllm` ; SGLang `issues?q=CPU+overhead+schedule`. What exists instead is shipped work (vLLM #23569/#24219) and specific sub-costs.
- **No quotable "async scheduling made no difference" measurement** exists anywhere reachable. Tried: `vllm async scheduling no improvement` ; `is:issue async-scheduling benchmark overhead` ; `"async scheduling" vllm regression` ; SGLang `overlap scheduler no gain`.
- **No engine or paper anywhere states that a reported speedup was within run-to-run noise, and none reports error bars on a speedup.** Tried: `"within noise" speedup LLM inference` ; `error bars throughput benchmark vllm` ; `is:issue benchmark variance noise vllm` ; `arxiv "run-to-run variance" inference serving`.
- **No withdrawn arXiv paper could be found on serving throughput, speculative decoding, KV cache, attention kernels, or inference quantization.** Tried: `arxiv withdrawn inference acceleration` ; `arxiv withdrawn LLM serving` ; `arxiv withdrawal quantization LLM` ; `arxiv "withdrawn" attention kernel`. Only two withdrawals were found in adjacent methodology territory (2406.18173, 2505.17460) and both are recorded with their verbatim withdrawal comments.
- **No reproducibility-challenge failure in LLM inference acceleration could be verified from a primary source.** Tried: `MLSys reproducibility challenge LLM inference failure` ; `NeurIPS reproducibility LLM serving` ; `KernelBench reproducibility failure`.
- **vLLM/SGLang have no `wontfix` and no `not planned` label at all** — both label queries return a literal "No results". Consequence, stated plainly: **refusal-shaped closure reasons live almost entirely in revert PRs**, which is why Section C is revert-weighted rather than `wontfix`-weighted.
- **Nothing was found on** `torch.cuda.set_sync_debug_mode`, per-step kernel-launch counts on the target rig, `PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF`/`expandable_segments` effect on step time, `cudaMalloc` in the hot path, `--ipc-only`, or `MQLLMEngine` serialization cost. Seven separate searches, all logged in S6's NEGATIVE FINDINGS. Anyone re-spending budget here should start from those.
- **vLLM `main` still has no grammar-compile worker cap and no FlashInfer sm120 preflight** — checked specifically so that no OPEN row was silently fixed between the 0.29.0 pin and `main` (V2).

### B2. waynehacking8 (contributor) in SGLang PR #27962 (no CI yet; maintainers Fridge003 / Qiaolin-Yu tagged, no `run-ci` label granted)

* **WHO is still asking:** waynehacking8 (contributor) in SGLang PR #27962 (no CI yet; maintainers Fridge003 / Qiaolin-Yu tagged, no `run-ci` label granted)
* **URL:** https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/27962
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "`context_attention_fwd` (`python/sglang/kernels/ops/attention/prefill_attention.py` after the kernel-tree move, the vision-attention / prefill path) picks `BLOCK = 128` for every CUDA capability > 8, but sm120 workstation/consumer Blackwell (RTX PRO 6000 / RTX 50xx) has only ~100KB shared memory per SM. The kernel fails to launch with: ``` triton.runtime.errors.OutOfResources: out of resource: shared memory, Required: 131072, Hardware limit: 101376 ``` crashing every caller of this path on sm120."
* **What specifically is missing:** A one-line sm120 carve-out (`BLOCK = 64`) in the SGLang Triton context/prefill attention kernel. Until it merges, SGLang's Triton attention prefill path — which is the *default* for models with attention sinks on sm120, and the documented workaround users are told to select — does not launch on this GPU at all. `#14311` fixed the same bug class in `extend_attention.py` and `prefill_attention.py` was missed.
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #27962; sibling test-only PR #27963 also open, both awaiting `run-ci`) — S3b queries: web_search "SGLang context_attention_fwd sm120 shared memory block size triton"; web_search "triton attention sm120 Blackwell issue block size"; direct fetch https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/27962
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

### B3. FlashAttention users on RTX 5090 / RTX PRO 6000 in Dao-AILab/flash-attention issue #2307, blocked on vLLM serving

* **WHO is still asking:** FlashAttention users on RTX 5090 / RTX PRO 6000 in Dao-AILab/flash-attention issue #2307, blocked on vLLM serving
* **URL:** https://github.com/Dao-AILab/flash-attention/issues/2307
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "### Blocker 1: Paged KV not supported ``` AssertionError: Paged KV not supported on SM 12.0 in this PR ``` The CuTe interface (`interface.py`) explicitly asserts `page_table is None` for SM120. vLLM uses paged KV cache for all production serving — without it, FA4 can't be used with vLLM's memory management."
* **What specifically is missing:** Kernel-level paged-KV support (and split-KV, and FP8 KV) for the SM120 CuTe FA forward kernel. `main` still carries the asserts: `assert page_table is None, "Paged KV not supported on SM 12.0 in this PR"`, `assert num_splits == 1, "SM120 forward only supports num_splits=1"`, `assert not is_split_kv, "SplitKV not supported on SM 12.0 in this PR"`, and the SM120 `can_implement()` accepts BF16/FP16 only. The reporter measures the cost of *not* having it: FlashInfer's native decode caps at `UNIFORM_SINGLE_TOKEN_DECODE`, forcing PIECEWISE CUDA graphs under spec decode, "~15% decode throughput".
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #2348 "Add SM120 kernel-level paged KV cache support"; split-KV PR #2336 still open; TMA successor PR #2599 still open) — S3b queries: direct fetch of https://github.com/Dao-AILab/flash-attention/pull/2348 (state OPEN), https://github.com/Dao-AILab/flash-attention/pull/2336 (OPEN), https://github.com/Dao-AILab/flash-attention/pull/2599 (OPEN); web_search "flash-attention pull request SM120 paged KV cache open 2026"
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

### B4. poorpaper (contributor) in SGLang issue #36531, who opened the fix PR; jhsmith409 reproduced it on a single RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96 GB

* **WHO is still asking:** poorpaper (contributor) in SGLang issue #36531, who opened the fix PR; jhsmith409 reproduced it on a single RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96 GB
* **URL:** https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues/36531
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "This is consistent with the docstring above `_resolve_trtllm_sparse_decode()` — the FA4 cute varlen fallback "runs a prefill-shaped kernel at decode row counts", so it appears to produce correct results only within the first KV page."
* **What specifically is missing:** On sm120 the backend resolver falls through to `flash_attn.cute.interface` / the vendored FA4 sm120 varlen dispatcher, which silently corrupts output past the first KV page. The reporter's positional measurement on the rig's exact GPU pinpoints it: "Using `/v1/completions` (no chat template, no reasoning/tool parsers involved) at `temperature=0`, with `--page-size 64` … absolute position **65**" and "With `--page-size 128` it moved to absolute position **193**". Short smoke tests pass; only prompts beyond one page degenerate. Two extra gates are needed for the working path: widen the sparse-decode gate so SM120 uses FlashInfer's `trtllm_batch_decode_with_kv_cache`, and fix the second `is_sm100_supported()` gate in `arg_groups/overrides.py::_qwen3_5_hybrid_overrides`, which "returns `{}` on SM120 and so never applies the family default".
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #36556 "fix(qsa): support SM120/SM121 sparse decode paths"; open PR #36644 "[Qwen3.8] Fix FP8 KV cache support in QSA") — S3b queries: direct fetch of https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/36556 (state OPEN) and https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/36644 (OPEN); web_search "sglang github pull closed unmerged attention backend sm120 wontfix"; web_search "SGLang attention backend sm120 fa4 not supported issue"
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

### B5. ZFXzzz (issue author, vLLM)

* **URL:** https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/56653
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "Is inclusive one-second bucket occupancy intentional for `max_concurrent_requests`, or should this field represent exact instantaneous concurrency? If the latter, I can prepare a small CPU-only regression test and a minimal fix."
* **What specifically is missing:** `vllm bench serve` reports `max_concurrent_requests` by incrementing integer-second buckets inclusively, so adjacent half-open request intervals report a peak of roughly 2x the configured `--max-concurrency` (observed 1→2, 2→4, 4→8, 8→16, 16→32). No maintainer has confirmed the intended metric contract, so the peak-concurrency figure in a saved bench JSON cannot currently be trusted as a saturation measurement.

The issue is still open with the author's offer to write the fix unanswered.

### B7. gpulost (PR author, SGLang)

* **WHO is still asking:** gpulost (PR author, SGLang)
* **URL:** https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/38868
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "An HTTP 200 SSE response can report a generation error after its headers have been sent. The serving benchmark currently counts these responses as successful for native /generate and OpenAI chat. An error before the first token even credits the requested maximum output length as generated tokens."
* **What specifically is missing:** SGLang's `bench_serving` still counts server-side streaming errors as successful requests and can credit the requested `max_tokens` as generated tokens, so failed requests pollute the success-throughput and latency aggregates. The fixing PR is open. On a single-GPU rig running at the edge of VRAM, spurious pre-first-token failures are exactly the ones that would be silently scored as full-length successes.
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #38868 — the same artifact; independently, `https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pulls?q=is%3Apr+is%3Aopen+benchmark+metric+fix` → 0 results, i.e. no competing fix).
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

### B18. vLLM core team, in the shipped v0.29.0 design document (a stated "Long term" plan with no ETA)

* **WHO is still asking:** vLLM core team, in the shipped v0.29.0 design document (a stated "Long term" plan with no ETA)
* **URL:** https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vllm-project/vllm/v0.29.0/docs/design/cuda_graphs.md
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "Long term, we've added the ability to partition the graph in Inductor instead of right after Dynamo. It can be enabled with `CompilationConfig.use_inductor_graph_partition=True` but is currently experimental and only available with `torch>=2.9`. This also increases compilation time as it has to compile the whole graph and cannot reuse piecewise compilation artifacts."
* **What specifically is missing:** `use_inductor_graph_partition` is the only path that lets whole-graph passes (attention fusion, sequence parallelism) coexist with piecewise CUDA graphs without recompiling twice, but it is experimental and slower to compile than the Dynamo-partition default — so the trade-off cannot be resolved by configuration alone. The 0.29.0 config also keeps a hard version gate: "use_inductor_graph_partition is only supported for torch>=2.9.0. Please set use_inductor_graph_partition=False instead."
* **S3b status:** NO-ACTIVE-WORK-FOUND (queries: `https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pulls?q=is%3Apr+is%3Aopen+inductor_graph_partition` → only #53447, a ROCm CI-matrix PR; `.../pulls?q=is%3Apr+is%3Aopen+graph+partition`; `web_search "vllm use_inductor_graph_partition open pull request 2026"`)
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

### B25. quocdat029x (contributor) — vLLM PR #56238 "[Bugfix][Metrics] Anchor queue time at the first QUEUED event"

* **WHO is still asking:** quocdat029x (contributor) — vLLM PR #56238 "[Bugfix][Metrics] Anchor queue time at the first QUEUED event"
* **URL:** https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56238
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "IterationStats.update_from_events overwrites queued_ts on every QUEUED event, but update_from_finished_request computes\n\n # Queued interval is from first QUEUED event to first SCHEDULED \n queued_time = req_stats . scheduled_ts - req_stats . queued_ts"
* **What specifically is missing:** The registered metric documents itself as "time spent in WAITING phase for request", but the computation subtracts a `queued_ts` that the event loop overwrites on every QUEUED event, so a request that re-enters the waiting queue (e.g. after preemption) reports a queue time anchored at its *last* queue entry rather than its first. There is no shipped per-step breakdown that separates time waiting for admission from time waiting for KV capacity from time waiting behind a long prefill.
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #56238; also open PR #55940 [Bugfix][Metrics] Record stats for aborted requests, open PR #56236 [Bugfix][Metrics] Exclude zero-token finish outputs from TTFT/ITL samples, open issue #55634 "[Usage]: aborted requests are counted in prompt_tokens_total but absent from every per-request metric").
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

* **URL:** https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues/38478
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "`Scheduler.get_new_batch_prefill` walks the waiting queue in FCFS order and `break`s at the first `AddReqResult.NO_TOKEN`. When the queue head is a large request whose KV budget does not fit, nothing behind it is considered, even if smaller requests (or requests with a long prefix hit, which need almost no new KV) would fit right now. ### B31. anencore94 (issue author and PR author); vLLM issue #49460 with open fix PR #49461

* **WHO is still asking:** anencore94 (issue author and PR author); vLLM issue #49460 with open fix PR #49461
* **URL:** https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/49460 ; https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49461
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "StructuredOutputManager sizes its grammar-compile ThreadPoolExecutor as max(1, (multiprocessing.cpu_count() + 1) // 2) with no upper bound. multiprocessing.cpu_count() is not cgroup-aware, so inside a container with a CFS quota (e.g. a Kubernetes pod with cpu limit 6 scheduled on a 172-core host) the pool is created with 86 workers. A burst of guided-JSON requests fans CPU-bound compiles out over the quota, sustains CFS throttling (ratio ≈ 1.00 for minutes in our production incident), and stalls the engine loop: inter-token latency collapsed from 7–9 ms to 68–93 ms with the GPU at 7–12% SM active, until requests hit the ingress deadline (intermittent 502s)."
* **What specifically is missing:** the sizing formula quoted in A5.8 is still uncapped and cgroup-unaware in the rig's vLLM, so on a 208-core host the pool is ~104 workers; the cap (`VLLM_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_MAX_COMPILE_WORKERS`) exists only in an unmerged PR. Separately, a JSON/GRAMMAR compile has no timeout at all (regex compilation does), so one deeply nested schema can occupy a compile worker indefinitely (issue #54003: compile time grows "roughly 2.5x per nesting level (super-exponential) with no bound"). EVIDENCE: READ BODY
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #49461 by the same author adds `VLLM_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_MAX_COMPILE_WORKERS`; independent searches `is:pr is:open grammar bitmask pinned allocation` (no successor for the bitmask allocation) and direct verification that #49461 is still open on its own page)
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

### B32. Are the remaining unpinned H2D staging copies in SGLang's `ForwardBatch.init_new` still stalling the scheduler thread on every batch?

* **WHO is still asking:** zhangxin81, SGLang PR #38703 (open, unmerged, awaiting maintainer benchmark request)
* **URL:** https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/38703
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "torch.tensor(host_list).to(device, non_blocking=True) without a pinned staging buffer is effectively a host-synchronous copy: per CUDA semantics, cudaMemcpyAsync to/from pageable host memory blocks the caller until the copy completes, so non_blocking=True is a no-op there and the calling thread (the scheduler, in this case) stalls on every such copy in the per-batch critical path."
* **What specifically is missing:** Five H2D sites in `forward_batch_info.py` are still unpinned and the author states "Not benchmarked here (no GPU on the dev machine used for this change)"; no maintainer has run the A/B on an H100 as offered.

* **URL:** https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/38760
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "Today, vLLM exposes Prometheus gauge/histogram metrics that are **scraped asynchronously** by an external collector. This has fundamental limitations for per-iteration telemetry: 1. **Lossy**: Prometheus scraping is pull-based at a configurable interval. With iteration times of 10-100ms, the scraper can miss 90%+ of iterations."
* **What specifically is missing:** No per-iteration engine-level metric exists. vLLM 0.29.0 has no Prometheus metric for scheduler time, sampling time, detokenization time, or CPU time per engine step, and a grep of every `"vllm:<name>"` string in `vllm/` (0.29.0) returns no such family. The only scheduler-timing signal in the tree is the OpenTelemetry span attribute `gen_ai.latency.time_in_scheduler` (`vllm/tracing/utils.py:38`), which is unavailable unless tracing is on. The RFC's own scoped subset — accurate GPU forward-pass `wall_time`, per-iteration identity/history, queue and KV-depth fields, ZMQ transport — is explicitly declared out of scope of the one draft PR that exists.
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #53550 "[Metrics] Add phase-aware engine iteration histograms" — draft, and open PR #52061 "[Feature] Add native forward-pass metrics emission"; both found by an independent search of the vLLM open-PR list, not from the RFC's own links)
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

### B39. yinjuncheng et al., vllm-project/vllm issue #47860 (labels: feature request, multi-modality) and sgl-project/sglang issue/PR track

* **WHO is still asking:** yinjuncheng et al., vllm-project/vllm issue #47860 (labels: feature request, multi-modality) and sgl-project/sglang issue/PR track
* **URL:** https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/47860
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "Today this time is invisible. Per the [metrics docs](https://docs.vllm.ai/en/stable/design/metrics/), `arrival_time` starts when tokenization begins, so all of the above is absorbed into TTFT. Operators serving vision/audio/video models cannot answer basic questions: - Is high TTFT caused by slow media downloads, slow CPU decode, or the engine?"
* **What specifically is missing:** The pre-engine slice of the request lifecycle — media download, media decode, item resolution, chat-template render, tokenize — has no Prometheus metric in vLLM 0.29.0. `arrival_time` is defined in `docs/design/metrics.md` as starting "when tokenization begins", so everything before that is folded into TTFT and cannot be attributed. `--limit-mm-per-prompt` bounds item counts but exposes no timing.
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #50875 "[Feature] Add Prometheus metrics for multimodal preprocessing pipeline" (same author, supersedes the closed-unmerged #50824), open PR #49682 "[Observability] Add multimodal media load metrics", open PR #36685 "Compute MM Pre-processing Timing Metrics in a Non-Blocking Way for Prod", and sgl-project/sglang open PR #38772 "[Feature] Add Prometheus metrics for multimodal preprocessing latency" — all found by independent open-PR-list searches)
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

### B40. Kangyan-Zhou (SGLang), PR #29527 motivation, sgl-project/sglang

* **WHO is still asking:** Kangyan-Zhou (SGLang), PR #29527 motivation, sgl-project/sglang
* **URL:** https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/29527
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "Part of that gap is delay that accrues before the engine starts its TTFT clock : the engine measures TTFT as received_time -> first token , but received_time is stamped inside the TokenizerManager process. If that process's asyncio event loop is starved (synchronous work, GIL contention, huge-prompt HF encoding), a request sits unstamped — invisible to engine-side TTFT but very visible to the client and the router."
* **What specifically is missing:** Neither engine's TTFT clock starts until after the front-end/tokenizer process has already consumed CPU time. The proposed metric (`sglang:event_loop_lag_seconds`) is not present in SGLang main (verified by grep of `python/sglang/`), and vLLM has no equivalent tokenizer/event-loop-lag metric at all — so on both stacks the client-visible pre-`received_time` delay is unmeasured.
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #29527 itself, plus open PR #35069 "feat(sgl-router): decompose the pre-first-token path into phase histograms" — both found by an independent search of the SGLang open-PR list)
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

### B42. SGLang maintainers, in the v0.5.19 quantization documentation ("Online Quantization" section)

* **WHO is still asking:** SGLang maintainers, in the v0.5.19 quantization documentation ("Online Quantization" section)
* **URL:** https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgl-project/sglang/v0.5.19/docs/docs/advanced_features/quantization.mdx
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "Our team is working on supporting more online quantization methods. SGLang will soon support methods including but not limited to"
* **What specifically is missing:** SGLang's `--quantization` path can requantize at load time for fp8 / w8a8 / nvfp4-style schemes, but cannot yet take an unquantized checkpoint and repack it into the AWQ/GPTQ/Marlin layouts at load time; today those layouts must arrive pre-baked in the checkpoint.

### B43. xikronz (contributor) and ProExpertProg (vLLM maintainer) — vllm-project/vllm issue #39428, open, label `help wanted`

* **WHO is still asking:** xikronz (contributor) and ProExpertProg (vLLM maintainer) — vllm-project/vllm issue #39428, open, label `help wanted`
* **URL:** https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/39428 ; https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/39480
* **Verbatim quote of the asking:** "This would likely require some work to fix weight loading for models like DeepSeek when `--hf-overrides.num_hidden_layers` is overriden."
* **What specifically is missing:** there is no `--num-hidden-layers` flag at all — the layer count is only reachable through the generic `--hf-overrides` dict — and when that override is used to slice a checkpoint, the weight loader is not guarded for the layers that no longer exist, so the model's stacked-parameter paths raise `KeyError`. The DeepSeek proof of concept (#39480) is still an unmerged draft, so no supported layer-slicing path exists for a dense model such as Qwen3-4B.
* **S3b status:** OCCUPIED (open PR #39480 "[CI/Build][Model][DeepSeek] Enabling partial layer configs for deepseek models" — state token `open`, read by direct fetch, independently of the issue that cites it; an issue comment had implied it landed).
* **Evidence:** READ BODY

- **C1** TensorRT-LLM issue #11799 "[Feature]: Compile trtllm-gen FMHA cubins for SM120/SM121 (consumer Blackwell)" — closed, `stateReason: COMPLETED` — No — this is an explicit vendor roadmap refusal, not a then-current technical blocker; the closing comment ("Closing per @pengbowang-nv's note above: trtllm-gen targets sm100/103 with no plan for SM120/121, and the XQA / FMHA_v2 fallback covers consumer Blackwell") is dated 2026-06 and TRT-LLM PR #15230 (2026, merged) confirms the fallback is still FMHA_v2, not trtllm-gen. ([link](https://github.com/NVIDIA/TensorRT-LLM/issues/11799))
- **C2** FA PR #2268 "Add SM120 (Blackwell GeForce / DGX Spark) flash attention" — CLOSED, unmerged — Yes — the stated reason was review granularity, not a technical blocker, and the split work largely **landed**: forward #2329, backward #2330 and varlen #2333 are all merged on main (see A1.14). What did *not* land is paged KV and split-KV, which are the parts that matter for serving (B1.2). ([link](https://github.com/Dao-AILab/flash-attention/pull/2268))
- **C3** FA PR #2325 "Fix SM80 forward+backward API drift in CuTe DSL kernels" — CLOSED, unmerged — Yes — tridao's own review note on the page was "Latest main has fixed some of this, can you rebase?", i.e. the drift was partly fixed upstream; the PR was closed for restructuring, not because the fix was wrong. - **C4** FA PR #2349 "Add SM120 TMA forward kernel with warp specialization" — CLOSED, unmerged — No — the successor (#2599) is still OPEN, so the SM120 TMA forward kernel is still not on main. ([link](https://github.com/Dao-AILab/flash-attention/pull/2349))
- **C5** FA PR #2336 "Add SM120 split-KV (FlashDecoding) with FP32 partial outputs" — paged-KV portion **removed** from the PR on review; PR itself still OPEN — No — the constraint still holds on main today: the SM120 branch still asserts `page_table is None, "Paged KV not supported on SM 12.0 in this PR"`, so the Python-level page-table gather that was rejected is still not the shipped design, and the kernel-level replacement (#2348) is still open. ([link](https://github.com/Dao-AILab/flash-attention/pull/2336))
- **C6** FA PR #2222 "[Draft][Cute,Fwd,Sm120] FA Cute DSL sm12x" — CLOSED, unmerged, was a draft — Yes — the dependency the author was waiting on has shipped (the FA issue threads reference `nvidia-cutlass-dsl 4.4.2`, and #2268's instructions require "nvidia-cutlass-dsl >= 4.4.1"), and SM120 CuTe attention is now on FA main via #2329/#2330/#2333, which is a different (merged) implementation than this draft. - **C117** pytorch/pytorch issue #133562 "FlexAttention with BLOCK_SIZE!=128" — partially — the note is about tensor-core granularity, but Blackwell consumer (sm120) has the same 128-granularity MMA, so the constraint still holds; however pytorch issue #195563 (still OPEN) now reports "On Blackwell, FA4 needs a block mask size in Q dimension must be a multiple of 256." — i.e. the *number* moved, not the limit. - **C136** microsoft/BitBLAS — archived Feb 24, 2026 — BitBLAS's hand-written TIR-script TensorCore transformations are replaced by TileLang's DSL — the method changed. ([link](https://github.com/microsoft/BitBLAS))
- **C137** `vllm/v1/attention/backends/fa_utils.py` — FA3/FA4 availability — This is the single highest-value expiry to watch: FA4's own paper targets sm100 only, and vLLM's comment says "restrict to SM100 for now". Checked at retrieval: neither has happened (the branch is still `major == 10`, and the FA4 paper's evaluation is B200-only). - **C139** `vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/indexer.py` — MXFP4 indexer KV dtype — Expires only if FlashInfer/vLLM add an MXFP4 indexer kernel for capability 12.x. - **C140** `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/online/nvfp4.py` — online per-token NVFP4 MoE — The gate is `is_device_capability_family(100)`; it expires when someone rewrites it to an explicit capability set (the pattern #56553 uses: `SM100/103`). At retrieval the direction of travel is *restriction to sm100*, not extension to sm120. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/v0.29.0/vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/online/nvfp4.py))
- **C141** vLLM issue #55397 — NVFP4 dense-linear kernel selection on SM12x — This is precisely the `is_device_capability_family(100)` mis-classification named in the brief. It expires when the first gate is rewritten; the sibling clause (`120 <= cc < 130`) shows maintainers already writing sm120-aware gates elsewhere in the same file, so a fix is a one-line change but is not in the retrieved tree. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/55397))
- **C142** vLLM PR #56553 — "[Bugfix][FlashInfer] Restrict W4A16 NVFP4 GEMM to SM100/103" — The regression is "un-tuned ... heuristic tactic" — it would expire if tuning data for sm120 were added upstream; at retrieval the chosen remedy is to exclude capability 12.x instead. Note the author's own caveat: this is a fork PR, not a maintainer-accepted decision. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/56553))
- **C143** `python/sglang/srt/layers/quantization/fp8_utils.py` — FlashInfer CuTe-DSL MXFP8 dense GEMM — Expires if FlashInfer ships a CuTe-DSL MXFP8 dense kernel for capability 12.x. The shipped vLLM docs make the same statement for MXFP8 generally: "Requires SM 100+ (Blackwell or newer) for w8a8, other GPUs use a w8a16 fallback". ([link](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/blob/v0.5.19/python/sglang/srt/layers/quantization/fp8_utils.py))
- **C144** vLLM issue #56702 — sm_120 missing from kernel-block-size branches (closed `not planned`) — The three block-size sites branch on `is_device_capability_family(90)`/family(100), so they expire when they gain an explicit `120` branch. The work is actively in flight elsewhere (#56509 was referenced as addressing the page-geometry faults), so this is a "watch" item rather than a permanent gap. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/56702))
- **C145** SGLang issue #39302 — "GitHub" title is "…GLM-5.x NoPE MLA … cannot run on SM120: every DSA sparse-MLA backend is unavailable" (title truncated in `fetch.py gh` as "[Bug] GLM-5.x NoPE MLA (qk_rope_head_dim=0) cannot run on SM120: every DSA sparse-MLA backend is unavailable") — Two independent constraints: (i) per-kernel instantiation gaps (fixable by adding sm120 kernels), and (ii) **99 KB opt-in shared memory vs ~228 KB on Hopper/B200** — a silicon-level difference that no software change removes. Any kernel whose tiles were sized against ~228 KB will not run on sm120 regardless of dispatch fixes. ([link](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues/39302))
- **C146** vLLM issue #54331 — GDN/linear-attention prefill backend on cc 12.0 — Same family-gate pattern; expires when the resolver gains a `120` branch. - **C147** vLLM issue #54225 — FlashInfer backend + NVFP4 + fp8 KV-cache crash on sm120 — Expires when the FlashInfer sm120 + NVFP4 + fp8-KV combination is fixed upstream; at retrieval the working configuration is the Triton fallback (i.e. the accelerated path is unavailable, exactly the pattern this section inventories). ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/54225))
- **C148** TensorRT-LLM issue #18039 — DeepSeek-V4-Flash NVFP4 on RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell — A third engine joins the pattern: the FMHA generation kernel for this model family is not wired for the consumer-Blackwell part. - **C149** blog — "Sakana walks back claims that its AI can dramatically speed up model training" (TechCrunch, 21 Feb 2025) — **BLOG**; corroborating **BLOG** GIGAZINE — The specific exploit (memory reuse) was patched in KernelBench v0.1 and in the robust-kbench harness; the general "reward metric can be gamed" constraint is not expired (C5.3). ([1](https://techcrunch.com/2025/02/21/sakana-walks-back-claims-that-its-ai-can-dramatically-speed-up-model-training/) ; [2](https://gigazine.net/gsc_news/en/20250225-sakana-ai-cuda-engineer-walks-back))
- **C150** PR #25093 "[Bug] Fix torch Compilation Cache Hit Error" (merged), PR #29558 "[Perf] Enable cuda graph for deepepHT, 5.3% throughput improvement, 4.4% TTFT improvement" (merged), PR #30910 "[BugFix] Partial revert of #29558 (DeepEP HT + PIECEWISE CG support)" (merged) — YES — the reason is bound to (a) the `(runtime_shape, graph_index, backend_name)` cache-key design as it existed in 2025 and (b) `deepep_high_throughput` with `data_parallel_size > 1`, which the target rig (1 GPU) cannot exercise at all. - **C156** PR #34666 "Revert "[BCG][5/N] MLA Fully Support"" — YES — reason is bound to the absorbed-MLA (MLA) path and to a >96 GB multi-GPU prefill pool on gfx950/CUDA dcp4; the rig's Qwen3-4B is non-MLA full attention on one sm120 GPU. ([link](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/34666))
- **C157** PR #37192 "[Feature] Support tc_piecewise prefill CUDA graphs under DP attention" — YES — the blockers are Dynamo/`fullgraph` tracing limits against a ctypes NCCL reduce-scatter plus a specific `coordinated_prefill` gate; both the `tc_piecewise` backend and DP-attention code paths were rewritten for 0.5.19's per-phase backend model, and the rig runs DP=1. ([link](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/37192))

([link](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/38354))

([link](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/32606))
- **C160** vllm-project/vllm issue #20221 — "[Bug]: Running Qwen-2.5-VL-72B-Instruct-FP8-Dynamic with vLLM 0.9.1/0.9.2-dev on an RTX 6000 Blackwell (96 GB) throws RuntimeError: Expected a.dtype() == torch::kInt8 to be true, but got false at torch.ops._C.cutlass_scaled_mm" — Yes, strongly — this is vLLM 0.9.2.dev311 on 2025-06-29, before sm120 had any dedicated FP8 CUTLASS sources. vLLM 0.29.0 now ships `scaled_mm_c3x_sm120.cu` / `scaled_mm_sm120_fp8.cu` / `scaled_mm_blockwise_sm120_fp8.cu` (A1.1, A1.2), so the FP8 arm of this failure is expected to be stale; the INT8 arm is *not* stale, because INT8 on sm120 is still hard-blocked by design (A1.3). ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/20221))
- **C161** sgl-project/sglang PR #38365 — "modelopt_fp4: skip the SiLU+FP4 fusion when the runner is marlin" (closed unmerged; no close-reason comment recoverable) — No — the defect is a wiring contradiction between a W4A4 fusion kernel and a W4A16 fallback backend, independent of GPU generation (reproduced on sm_120 and structurally applies to any sm80-sm90 device that takes the Marlin NVFP4 fallback). ([link](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/38365))
- **C163** vLLM issue #42185 "[RFC]: Cache-affinity-aware request ordering for the V1 scheduler" — a `CacheAffinityScheduler` plugin loadable via `--scheduler-cls`; RFC closed, PR never opened — The measured +0.2% result was taken on **Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct on L40S** against the `AsyncScheduler` of that date. The RFC's own "When This Would Help" section names the untested regime — "Multi-tenant RAG with N distinct system prompts where N > KV cache capacity / prompt_blocks — forcing eviction competition between tenant prefixes". On a single 96 GB card with a 4B model the KV pool is very large relative to a 4B model's per-token footprint, so the eviction-competition precondition is *even less* likely to hold than on L40S; the reason is therefore unlikely to have expired in the direction that would revive this. Separately, the successor design (#53277, still open) narrowed the target to cache-warm continuation under long-context contention. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/42185))
- **C164** vLLM issue #30256 "[RFC]: SLA-Tiered Scheduling for Latency/Throughput Optimization" — proposed `sla_tier: Optional[Literal["interactive", "batch", "background"]] = "batch"` on `SamplingParams`, `max_interactive_batch_tokens`, and tier-aware preemption — The RFC's motivating condition is "Without differentiation, interactive requests can be queued behind large batch jobs, hurting quality of service." That condition still holds in v0.29.0 — `SchedulerConfig` still exposes only `policy: "fcfs" \| "priority"` with a single integer priority and no tier concept. The blocker was not a changed constraint, it was lack of implementation. ([1](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/42185) ; [2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/30256))
- **C165** vLLM issue #24484 "[RFC]: Enhancing the Pluggable Scheduler with a Workload-Aware Adaptive Policy"; successor PR #33392 (still open) — The "11x prefill slowdown with chunked prefill enabled" report is undated against a vLLM version in the thread, and vLLM V1 shipped chunked prefill *on by default* with decode-prioritised ordering (A.4.1) after this report, plus `scheduler_reserve_full_isl` (see C.4.6). The specific thrashing explanation offered in the thread — re-admitting a chunked prefill without checking that the whole context fits — is exactly what `scheduler_reserve_full_isl` now guards, so the mechanism most likely to have produced that number has a shipped mitigation in v0.29.0. ([1](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/24484) ; [2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/33392) ; [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21758))
- **C166** SGLang issue #36131 "[Performance] Unified-cache default flip regresses long-prefix decode throughput on Spark and Thor" (closed, `STATE_REASON: COMPLETED`); the flip came from SGLang PR #34653 — The measurement is on **DGX Spark and NVIDIA Thor (both 1 GPU, TP1)** with `openai/gpt-oss-20b` MXFP4 and `nvidia/Gemma-4-31B-IT-NVFP4`. The regression is attributed to unified-cache/SWA behaviour and to `chunked_prefill_size` 8192 not dividing the 32,000-token prefix — and 8192 is *also* the SGLang 0.5.19 auto-default chosen for a 96 GB card (A.4.11). The affected models are hybrid sliding-window-attention models, whereas Qwen3-4B is dense full attention, so the SWA-tombstone mechanism named in the root cause does not apply to this rig. The reason has therefore very likely expired *for this rig's model*, while the chunk-boundary observation about `chunked_prefill_size` remains live. ([link](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues/36131))
- **C167** vLLM PR #21809 "[Bugfix] Scheduler: only schedule prefill chunks when entire context fits" (state token `CLOSED`; not merged) — The reason has expired. PR #21809 was written against a V1 scheduler that admitted a chunk without checking whether the request's whole context would fit; vLLM v0.29.0 ships exactly that check as `scheduler_reserve_full_isl: bool = True`, default on. The thrash cycle described in the PR body is guarded against by default in the rig's version. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/21809))
- **C168** PR #44405 "fix: default-disable FlashInfer sampler on consumer Blackwell GPUs (SM120/SM121)" — YES — the abandonment rests on the flashinfer 0.6.13-era kernel: #47669 states "0.6.14 is the first release containing the fix; current pin (0.6.13) is affected", and vLLM 0.29.0 ships the sampler enabled by default again (A5.1). The residual sm120 risk is now a toolkit/pinning constraint (B5.2), not the original kernel race. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/44405))
- **C169** PR #11636 "[V1] [7/N] API Server: Multiprocessing Detokenizer" — YES — the measurement was taken against the Python V0/V1 frontend of early 2025 ("Current main API server is within 3% of the LLM performance already"); the 3-process split is a premise of the later Rust-frontend work, so the 3% margin is not a statement about the 2026 frontend. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/11636))
- **C170** PR #8975 "Fix: Enable jump-forward optimization for Outlines backend"; PR #4032 "Misc clean up; Remove the support of jump forward" — YES — the outlines backend is no longer the default path (SGLang defaults `--grammar-backend xgrammar`, A5.13), so the crash-prone outlines jump-forward code is off the default route; the feature-level removal (#4032) still stands for every backend.
