So as it stands the routing sends consumer Blackwell to a kernel that can't run there." ([link](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/pull/2598))
- **C242** vLLM issue #20098 — "[RFC]: Lazy CUDA Graph capture" — Expires on lazy/partial capture shipping; the RFC is the active work. For a single 4B model the absolute capture time is small, so the practical weight of this item on this rig is low — recorded because the brief asked for CUDA-graph negatives and because it is the *verified* form of that negative (no primary source was found showing decode-time graphs slower than eager for a Qwen3-4B-class dense model at batch 1). ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/20098))
- **C243** pytorch/pytorch issue #179697 ; sgl-project/sglang issue #1008 — Expires when Inductor's small-batch codegen beats eager for these shapes; the reports show max-autotune is *not* the fix at bs=1 (0.0843 ms, still slower than eager 0.0792 ms). ([1](https://github.com/pytorch/pytorch/issues/179697) ; [2](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues/1008))
- **C244** sgl-project/sglang issue #21855 — "Replace memory-bound custom CUDA kernels with torch.compile generated fusions" — The fusion's sign flips with batch (−5.6% at B=1 → positive at B≥32), so it expires for a high-concurrency server; for a single-stream latency rig the fusion is a regression. The remaining −2.0% at B=1 with the split graph is a second, smaller regression that is not attributed. ([link](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues/21855))
- **C245** vLLM issue #28234 — "Online Dynamic FP8 Quantization (`--quantization="fp8"`) is slower than BF16/FP16 on RTX 5090" — The cause is per-forward dynamic scaling, which expires if a calibrated/static activation scale is used instead — i.e. the fix is a configuration change (static scales), not a hardware change. Measured cost on that part: throughput −17.2% (3370.29 → 2791.53 tokens/s), wall time +17.9%. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/28234))
- **C246** vLLM issue #37618 — "FP8 kv cache on b200 with qwen3.5 has degraded accuracy" — The culprit is a *kernel's* scale handling, not the dtype: in the same report `VLLM_USE_DEEP_GEMM=0` restores GSM8K 0.8302 from 0.7089, while `--enforce-eager` (0.7210) and `--moe-backend triton` (0.7392) do not recover it. Expires when the DeepGEMM E8M0 path is fixed; that is also the path sm120 currently mis-gates (C3.5/C3.8 family). ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/37618))
- **C247** Issue #55722 "[Bug]: `fuse_attn_quant` without `use_inductor_graph_partition` drops all piecewise cudagraphs on backends that cannot do FULL (-38 % on ROCm sparse MLA)" — no — the unreachable `FULL_AND_PIECEWISE` recovery branch is still in 0.29.0, and the retraction explicitly leaves the small-model/CPU-bound case unmeasured, which is the target rig's regime (Qwen3-4B, one GPU, launch-overhead-sensitive decode). ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/55722))
- **C248** PR #32782 "[CUDA graph] Disable prefill CUDA graph for the whole trtllm_mla backend family, not just DeepSeek (fixes #32655)" — YES — reason is scoped to the `trtllm_mla` backend family and to MLA models (Kimi-K2.x / DeepSeekV3); the rig runs dense full attention, and the backend family mapping has changed in 0.5.19. ([1](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/32782) ; [2](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues/32655))
- **C249** arXiv:2606.13233 "ReSET: Accurate Latency-Critical NVFP4 Reasoning via Step-Aware Temperature Scaling" — No — the small-batch regime is exactly the rig's regime (single GPU, batch 1 decode), and the finding is a kernel-family property (tensor cores idle at M=1), not a version artefact. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13233))
- **C250** arXiv:2405.04532 "QServe: W4A8KV4 Quantization and System Co-design for Efficient LLM Serving" — Yes — measured on A100/L40S (Ampere/Ada) before Marlin/Machete/b12x existed; the 20-90% dequant overhead figure is explicitly a property of the dequant-on-CUDA-cores designs of that era. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04532))
- **C251** arXiv:2409.11055 "Exploring the Trade-Offs: Quantization Methods, Task Difficulty, and Model Size in Large Language Models From Edge to Giant" — Partially — the study predates NVFP4/NVFP4-QAT recipes (e.g. arXiv:2609.04098's W4A4/QAT-class results). The model-size trend itself is a scaling observation, not a kernel artefact, so it is the one row here most likely to constrain the Qwen3-4B target directly. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11055))
- **C252** arXiv:2505.19433 "Can Compressed LLMs Truly Act? An Empirical Evaluation of Agentic Capabilities in LLM Compression" — No — the benchmark measures task behaviour, not kernels; but the weights tested are GPTQ/AWQ W4A16-class, so a NVFP4/QAT recipe is not covered by it. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19433))
- **C253** arXiv:2606.08761 "APEX4: Efficient Pure W4A4 LLM Inference via Intra-SM Compute Rebalancing" — Yes, for the specific GPUs — Ampere/Ada only, and the paper's own conclusion is that viability is platform-dependent. sm120's ρ is not published in any artifact found, which is precisely the missing measurement for this rig. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08761))
- **C254** PR #39592 "[Pooling] Disable async scheduling by default for pooling models" (commit ee3c0c83db8) — YES — the statement is explicitly scoped to "as currently implemented", and the same thread points at MRV2's double buffering as a design that "might actually benefit the prefill stage"; on a decode-dominated single-user workload the sign may differ. ([1](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/39592) ; [2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/32140))
- **C255** Issue #56126 "RFC: enable dual-batch overlap (DBO) for dense TP models at DP=1" — yes — the author's own conclusion names the changed constraint: "on this box, at b ≤ 128, it does not [pay]" because "at these shapes the per-rank GEMMs are weight-streaming bound, not compute bound", and predicts it "should pay where the per-rank GEMM is compute-bound — larger batch, larger hidden per rank, or fewer TP ranks". The target rig is *fewer* TP ranks (one GPU) but also much smaller batch. ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/56126))
- **C256** Issue #52746 "[ray.serve.llm] serve.llm with streaming has overhead compared to vllm-v0 for a single replica when concurrency > 32" ; Issue #59681 "Additional Overhead in Ray Serve LLM than Bare vLLM images" — Partly. The dominant fixed cost in #59681 was the response `Batcher` waiting out `MODEL_RESPONSE_BATCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 50 ms); the reporter measured that setting it to 0 drops TTFT p50 64.8 → 26.3 ms, and #64534 was opened to flush the first result immediately. The maintainer's remedy ("direct streaming") changes the recommended configuration, so the *default* numbers in these issues may no longer reproduce. The residual high-concurrency tail (TTFT 71 → 330 ms at c=32, 114 → 758 ms at c=64, throughput 5518 → 3634 tok/s) was explicitly flagged by the reporter as "architectural ... rather than a single fixable defect" and remained open at the time of the thread. ([1](https://github.com/ray-project/ray/issues/52746) ; [2](https://github.com/ray-project/ray/issues/59681))
- **C257** triton-inference-server/server issue #8515 "vLLM backend multi-gpu one instance performance" — The reason names CPU exhaustion under a Python-backend shm path; that is an architectural property of the Triton Python backend, so it does not obviously expire. The specific numbers are two-GPU, so they do not transfer to the target rig. ([link](https://github.com/triton-inference-server/server/issues/8515))
- **C258** `PassConfig.enable_qk_norm_rope_fusion` (implemented by #27018/#27165, see A9.4); issue #34391 "[Performance]: qknorm+rope fusion slower than unfused on H100" — likely yes — the stated regression is explicitly measured "on H100" (SM90, 132 SMs) and the reporter's own hardware note is "H100-80G-PCIe"; the rig is sm120 with a different SM count and an L2/HBM configuration, and a later comment in the same issue reports numbers from a B300 ("only happen on h card... sad"). Nobody has published a sm120 measurement. ([1](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vllm-project/vllm/v0.29.0/docs/design/fusions.md) ; [2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/34391))
- **C259** PR #40171 "[Kernel] [Helion] Force disable HOP path due to performance regression" — yes, plausibly — the disabling PR calls itself "temporarily", the mechanism is a frozen-config bug rather than a hardware property, and Helion fused-quant kernels were later retuned (merged #48797 "[Kernel][Helion] Disable warp specialization in rms_norm_per_block_quant B200 configs", merged #48900/#48893 "[Kernel][Helion] Retune grouped quant schedules" / "Optimize cache policies for dynamic quant kernels"). ([link](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/40171))
- **C260** Issue #25094 "[Performance]: Compiled `QuantFP8.forward_native` group quantization (1, 128) slower than CUDA on H100/RTX5090"; tracked in #25689's checklist as "enable torch group quant by default ... don't know root cause for perf slowdown yet" — yes — the reason is explicitly torch-version-dependent (the reporter's own edit asks for a re-measurement on torch 2.10/2.11), and the v0.29.0 tag still ships `IS_QUANTIZED = False` rather than the torch-group-quant default the checklist wanted. ([1](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/25094) ; [2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/25689))
- **C261** arXiv 2608.26389 — "LowRankArena: A Standardized Evaluation Platform for SVD-Based LLM Compression" — No expiry identified — the hybrid dense-fallback policy is a property of the compression method, not of a hardware generation or engine version. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26389) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.26389v1))
- **C262** arXiv 2606.04511 — "SparDA: Sparse Decoupled Attention for Efficient Long-Context LLM Inference" — No — the justification offered ("prefill is compute-bound, so batch size does not matter") is an architectural assumption that is exactly what fails on a single 96 GB sm120 card running a 4B dense model, where prefill at batch 4 is nowhere near GPU-saturating. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04511) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.04511v1))
- **C263** arXiv 2605.18753 — "DashAttention: Differentiable and Adaptive Sparse Hierarchical Attention" — **Yes.** The stated motivation is that CUDA graphs mitigate BS=1 kernel-launch cost — a vLLM/SGLang-version-dependent claim. On the rig's vLLM 0.29.0 with piecewise/full CUDA graphs, "batch size 1 is dominated by kernel-launch overhead" is the thing to re-measure, not to inherit. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18753) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.18753v1))
- **C264** arXiv 2604.09613 — "Token-Budget-Aware Pool Routing for Cost-Efficient LLM Inference" — **Yes.** The paper's own model omits "CUDA graph boundaries", which are a first-class, version-specific cost on modern vLLM; A100-80GB is also a different memory/compute regime from a single 96 GB sm120 card. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09613) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.09613v1))
- **C265** arXiv 2604.02344 — "Characterizing WebGPU Dispatch Overhead for LLM Inference Across Four GPU Vendors, Three Backends, and Three Browsers" — No — the run-to-run disagreement between the paper's own two reports of the same configuration is a property of the measurement, not of the hardware generation. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02344) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.02344v1))
- **C266** arXiv 2504.11750 — "Characterizing and Optimizing LLM Inference Workloads on CPU-GPU Coupled Architectures" — **Yes.** "Fully synthesized inference graphs" is exactly what vLLM 0.29.0's CUDA-graph modes already do at runtime, and the 1.32x max-autotune number is against an eager baseline on an H100 over PCIe — three axes (graph mode, GPU generation, host interconnect) that all differ on the rig. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11750) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2504.11750v1))
- **C267** arXiv 2606.11690 — "Beyond Per-Token Pricing: A Concurrency-Aware Methodology for LLM Infrastructure Cost Estimation" — No. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11690) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.11690v1))
- **C268** arXiv 2608.23806 — "Giga-Embeddings: Mixture-of-Experts Encoders for High-Throughput Text Embeddings" — No. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23806) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.23806v1))
- **C269** arXiv 2609.05512 — "Reasoning-Aware Compression: Identifying and Protecting Vulnerable Reasoning Circuits for Energy-Efficient LLM Deployment" — **Yes, strongly.** V100-SXM2 (sm70, 32 GB, no BF16) is two architecture generations and a form-factor away from a Blackwell sm120 96 GB card; every energy and quantization-packing conclusion is regime-specific. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05512) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.05512v1))
- **C270** arXiv 2609.11133 — "Phase-Decoupled, Model-Calibrated Power Control for Disaggregated LLM Serving" — **Yes.** The results are B200 + MoE (480B/235B) under disaggregated serving; the rig is one sm120 card running a dense 4B model, and the paper's own "decode cap carried all of the run-to-run variance" is the concrete warning that on a *single workstation card* the power cap is the knob that injects variance into the measurement. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11133) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11133v1))
- **C271** arXiv 2507.19427 — "Step-3 is Large yet Affordable: Model-system Co-design for Cost-effective Decoding" — **Yes.** 20 tokens/s SLA on H800/A800/H20 clusters is a fabric-bound regime; the paper's own cost model assumes AFD attention and FFN can each "operate near hardware limitations", which is not a single-card statement. (See also D2.) ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19427) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2507.19427v1))
- **C272** arXiv 2604.07815 — "AsyncTLS: Efficient Generative LLM Inference with Asynchronous Two-level Sparse Attention" — **Yes.** The 4.70x is a throughput ratio against a baseline *that could only run at BS=1 because of memory*; on a 96 GB card the full-attention baseline is not memory-constrained to BS=1 at 96K for a 4B/8B dense model, so the ratio is an artifact of the baseline's VRAM, not of the method's speed. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07815) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.07815v1))
- **C273** arXiv 2602.02180 — "STILL: Selecting Tokens for Intra-Layer Hybrid Attention to Linearize LLMs" — **Yes.** "Softmax attention becomes infeasible beyond 16K tokens" is a statement about the paper's own H200 memory budget under a specific implementation; the rig's 96 GB and vLLM 0.29.0 paged attention change where that cliff sits. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02180) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.02180v1))
- **C274** arXiv 2604.09557 — "SPEED-Bench: A Unified and Diverse Benchmark for Speculative Decoding" — No — the GIL is still there. This is the clearest published acknowledgement that a *client-side* limit can cap a measured server throughput, which is exactly the "what the harness silently excludes (client-side latency)" hazard. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09557) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.09557v1))
- **C275** arXiv 2603.10031 — "Architecture-Aware LLM Inference Optimization on AMD Instinct GPUs: A Comprehensive Benchmark and Deployment Study" — **Yes, twice over.** (a) Software: the paper itself says its "architecture-specific configuration findings (block size, AITER flags) are version-dependent and should be re-validated on future releases", and it ran vLLM v0.14.1 while the rig runs vLLM 0.29.0. (b) Hardware: the study's default configuration is TP = 8 on MI325X — an eight-GPU ROCm node, not a single sm120 card. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10031) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2603.10031v1))
- **C276** arXiv 2608.22048 — "More Accurate or More Efficient? Evaluating Locally Deployed Compact Open-Weight Language Models for Mathematical Reasoning" — No. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22048) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.22048v1))
- **C277** arXiv 2306.00978 — "AWQ: Activation-aware Weight Quantization for LLM Compression and Acceleration" (v1 1 Jun 2023; last revised 25 Apr 2026, v6) — **Yes, in both directions.** (a) Hardware: the A100/2-4xA100 clause is a 2023 rig; the rig's single card has more VRAM than the "larger models" configuration used. (b) Quantization formats: the paper explicitly does not study FP4/FP8, which are the formats the rig's sm120 generation actually accelerates natively — so its negative findings about data types are superseded. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.00978) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2306.00978v1))
- **C278** arXiv 2602.11937 — "Extending Puzzle for Mixture-of-Experts Reasoning Models with Application to GPT-OSS Acceleration" — **Yes.** The single-GPU number is derived from an 8-GPU-node constraint, on an 88B **MoE** whose parent is "memory-limited to batch size 1" at 64K — none of which holds for a 4B dense model on 96 GB. Also MoE, which the rig's target model is explicitly not. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11937) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.11937v1))
- **C279** arXiv:2510.05175 — "Exact Causal Attention with 10% Fewer Operations" (v3, 11 Oct 2025) — The blocking constraint is *algorithmic* (FLOP-reduction requires materialising intermediates that IO-aware kernels deliberately never materialise), not a compute-capability gate; nothing about sm120 or a newer FA would remove it. No sm120/CUDA-13 change found that revives it. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05175))
- **C280** arXiv:2609.04105 — "Hardware-Aware FP4 FlashAttention-4" (3 Sep 2026) — Would expire if the softmax/conversion middle stage stopped dominating, e.g. on sm100 B300/GB300 (the paper notes "B300 and GB300 GPUs have doubled the exponential throughput to 32 ops / clock / SM, though these GPUs are not yet widely available at the time of writing"). **sm120 is not that hardware** and is not addressable by this method at all today, because FA4-class kernels are gated to capability family 100 in vLLM. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04105))
- **C281** arXiv:2606.04511 — "SparDA: Sparse Decoupled Attention for Efficient Long-Context LLM Inference" (3 Jun 2026) — The regression is batch-size dependent (the paper reports the full pipeline "pulls ahead of the no-prefetch variant" by B16 and is ~40% faster by B64), so the limitation would expire for a serving deployment at B≥16 — but *not* for the single-stream, batch-1 target of this rig. No sm120-specific revision found. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04511))
- **C282** arXiv:2606.26666 — "PersistentKV: Page-Aware Decode Scheduling for Long-Context LLM Serving on Commodity GPUs" (v2, 1 Jul 2026; 7-page workshop paper) — The claim is scoped by the authors to "commodity GPUs" and to a roofline-style isolated benchmark; it would expire if a scheduling-layer integration (not a kernel) were shipped in vLLM/SGLang. None found — the decode-kernel lead in these engines remains FlashInfer/XQA. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26666))
- **C283** arXiv:2605.16928 — "Full Attention Strikes Back: Transferring Full Attention into Sparse within Hundred Training Steps" (v2, 8 Jun 2026) — The bound is a bandwidth argument (sparse selection still streams a large KV working set), so it would only expire with a change in the memory system or a harder sparsity budget — not with sm120. Note the paper also *requires a training phase* (head specialisation is learned), which is itself a limitation class the brief asked about. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16928))
- **C284** arXiv:2607.08786 — "Accelerating GPU Inference of Large Language Models with Moderately Unstructured Sparse Weight Matrices" (13 Jun 2026) — The paper's own diagnosis is that the *format* is the problem, and it notes a compute-bound regime inversion (slower than cuBLAS). A denser/sparser hardware path (e.g. block-scaled FP4 tensor cores on sm100/sm120) could change the regime; no work found that ports this scheme onto it. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08786))
- **C285** arXiv:2602.04541 — "LycheeDecode: Accelerating Long-Context LLM Inference via Hybrid-Head Sparse Decoding" (4 Feb 2026) — Two dependencies: (i) the method requires a *training phase* to learn per-head sparsity (its own §4.2), and (ii) integration into a serving engine is absent. Would expire only on a training+integration effort; nothing found. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04541))
- **C286** arXiv:2607.25291 — "CoSA: Accelerating Long-Context Inference via Proxy-Kernel Co-Designed Sparse Attention" (v2, 7 Aug 2026) — A decode-side proxy+kernel must be written; the ACCELERATION the paper claims is TTFT-only ("it reduces TTFT by 2.53 × \times at 128K context"), so for a single-stream decode-bound rig the benefit does not exist yet. No sm120-specific port found. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25291))
- **C287** arXiv:2606.07713 — "Attention at the Theoretical Minimum: A Mathematics of Arrays Framework for Memory-Optimal Transformer Kernels" (5 Jun 2026) — The paper is a theory/derivation framework whose own roadmap places validation on Frontier/Aurora/El Capitan (exascale machines) — hardware this rig cannot be — and notes gains "cannot be ... ported". Nothing to port to sm120 today. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07713))
- **C288** arXiv:2605.30571 — "Memory-Bound but Not Bandwidth-Limited: The Physical AI Inference Gap in Batch-1 LLM Decode" (28 May 2026) — The finding is explicitly about launch-side overhead which "becomes visible on fast GPUs but remains mostly hidden on slower, bandwidth-bound GPUs" — the *opposite* expiry condition from a hardware upgrade: a faster GPU makes it worse, not better. Its scope excludes sm120 (testbed is H100/A100-80GB/L40S/L4), so on this rig the finding is unverified rather than expired. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30571))
- **C289** arXiv:2607.16241 — "KernelBench-Verified: Do LLM-Generated Kernels Actually Beat PyTorch?" — The gap is a *measurement* gap (baseline TF32 setting + hidden test distributions), not hardware; it expires when the standard KernelBench protocol adopts the realistic baseline. This is a headline-number downward revision (1.43× → 0.88×) with no arXiv Comments-field record — see NEGATIVE FINDINGS. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16241))
- **C290** arXiv:2509.14279 — "Towards Robust Agentic CUDA Kernel Benchmarking, Verification, and Optimization" (robust-kbench) — The contaminated-task set is what expires (already excluded in v2); the underlying reward-hacking surface of KernelBench is not fixed in general (C5.3, C5.5). ([link](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.14279v1))
- **C291** arXiv:2607.04454 — "Correct but Slow: An Empirical Study of the GPU Kernel Evaluation Gap in Modern Domain-Specific Languages" — Pass/fail gates that ignore a slowdown bound; expires when evaluation adds a lower-bound on speed. Nothing found that changes this at retrieval. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04454))
- **C292** arXiv:2507.11948 — "Kevin: Multi-Turn RL for Generating CUDA Kernels" flash-attention issue #1572 — "flash-attention-3 batch decode is significantly slower than flashinfer on H20" — Harness ordering only; the paper's own speedups are additionally scoped — "the speedups we measure in Section 3.2 are only accurate for those dimensions on our NVIDIA H200 GPUs" — and its headline is modest (mean speedup "from 0.53x to 1.10x of baseline (PyTorch Eager)"). The measurement is on H20 (a Hopper-class part) for MHA decode; the rig's Qwen3-4B is GQA, where the same report finds parity — hence held out of the count. ([link](https://arxiv.org/html/2507.11948v1 https://github.com/Dao-AILab/flash-attention/issues/1572))
- **C293** arXiv:2509.23202 "Bridging the Gap Between Promise and Performance for Microscaling FP4 Quantization" — No — the two mechanism claims are format-level (NVFP4 uses block 16, MXFP4 uses a power-of-two E8M0 scale), and both formats are unchanged on sm120 hardware. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23202))
- **C294** arXiv:2608.25188 "Transforms for LLM Quantization: The Great Inversion and Format Co-Design"; arXiv:2609.08135 "KBBQ: A Predictive Noise Law and the Limits of Spectrum Flattening in FP4 Quantization" — No — this is a mathematical ceiling on the method class, not a hardware result. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25188) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08135))
- **C295** arXiv:2605.20315 "Mix-Quant: Quantized Prefilling, Precise Decoding for Agentic LLMs" — No — the finding is about where quantization error is tolerable, independent of GPU generation. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20315))
- **C296** arXiv:2405.16406 "SpinQuant: LLM quantization with learned rotations" — Partially — the 2.9-point figure is LLaMA-2 7B with INT4 W4A4 + KV4 in 2024, not NVFP4 W4A4. The qualitative result (rotation alone does not make W4A4 lossless) is corroborated for FP4 by C15's ceiling. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16406))
- **C297** arXiv 2410.13276 "SeerAttention: Learning Intrinsic Sparse Attention in Your LLMs" (title re-verified via `fetch.py arxiv 2410.13276`) — PARTLY — "≈40 A100 GPU hours" is A100-era framing and would be cheaper on the rig, but the blocking property (a *trained* sparsity gate must exist per model before the kernel helps) does not expire. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13276))
- **C298** arXiv 2407.02490 "MInference 1.0: Accelerating Pre-filling for Long-Context LLMs via Dynamic Sparse Attention" (title re-verified) — PARTLY — the A100-only *measurement* is A100-era and expired as a fact, but the blocker is the three hand-written kernels, which have no Blackwell/sm120 build or CI that I could find (see NEGATIVE FINDINGS). ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02490))
- **C299** arXiv 2408.07092 "Post-Training Sparse Attention with Double Sparsity" (title re-verified; note the id is right but the earlier working title in the brief was wrong) — PARTLY — the A10G half of the evaluation shows the idea is not A100-locked, but the offline-calibration requirement (a per-model calibration artifact) does not expire, and no sm120/Blackwell evaluation exists. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07092))
- **C300** arXiv 2502.11089 "Native Sparse Attention: Hardware-Aligned and Natively Trainable Sparse Attention" (title re-verified) — NONE for the paper's own numbers (8-GPU A100 evaluation is a scale constraint, not a hardware-era one), and none for the engine side — NSA remains model-native (MLA/DSA) rather than a switchable optimization. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11089))
- **C301** arXiv 2502.13189 "MoBA: Mixture of Block Attention for Long-Context LLMs" (title re-verified) — UNKNOWN — the paper does not state a hardware constraint precise enough to expire; MoBA-as-shipped lives in Kimi's stack, not in either engine on the rig. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13189))
- **C302** arXiv 2406.10774 "Quest: Query-Aware Sparsity for Efficient Long-Context LLM Inference" (title re-verified) — PARTLY — the RTX 4090 evaluation is the *opposite* of an A100-only constraint, so "consumer GPU" is not the blocker; what remains is that Quest's paged top-k selection has no upstream implementation in either engine. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10774))
- **C303** arXiv 2309.00071 "YaRN: Efficient Context Window Extension of Large Language Models" (title re-verified) — NO — the fine-tuning requirement is a property of the method, not of the hardware. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.00071))
- **C304** arXiv 2402.13753 "LongRoPE: Extending LLM Context Window Beyond 2 Million Tokens" (title re-verified) — NO — the 1k-step fine-tuning requirement per model is method-inherent. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13753))
- **C305** arXiv 2310.05209 "Scaling Laws of RoPE-based Extrapolation" (title re-verified) — NO — training-length requirement is method-inherent. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.05209))
- **C306** arXiv 2004.05150 "Longformer: The Long-Document Transformer" (title re-verified; BigBird 2007.14062 is the same family — sparse local+global pattern baked in at pretraining time) — YES, in a specific sense — the paper's constraint ("you must pretrain the pattern in") is exactly why the 2025-26 answer is *model-native* interleaved SWA (Gemma-3, GPT-OSS) rather than a runtime flag; the runtime-flag version is what `--hf-overrides` cannot deliver (A7.2). ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.05150))
- **C307** arXiv:2403.17887 — "The Unreasonable Ineffectiveness of the Deeper Layers"; arXiv:2403.03853 — "ShortGPT: Layers in Large Language Models are More Redundant Than You Expect" — yes — both measurements are on Llama-2 / Llama-3 / Baichuan-2 class models of 2024 vintage; neither paper tests Qwen3-4B, and neither is a statement about vLLM or sm120. ([1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17887) ; [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03853))
- **C308** arXiv:2404.16710 — "LayerSkip: Enabling Early Exit Inference and Self-Speculative Decoding" — no — the reason is that the LM head was never trained to unembed from intermediate layers, which is a property of the pretrained checkpoint, not of the hardware or the serving stack. (The paper itself notes that a different self-speculative approach "does not require changing a model's weights", so the constraint is specific to its trained early-exit heads.) ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16710))
- **C309** arXiv:2606.21906 — "Deeper is Not Always Better: Mitigating the Alignment Tax via Confident Layer Decoding", section on the limitations of static early exit — yes — what is abandoned is the *static/fixed* exit layer applied uniformly; the same paper reports that a dynamic entropy-selected near-final layer recovers accuracy, and it presents that method as an accuracy intervention rather than a throughput win, so this row must not be read as "early exit does not work". ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21906))
- **C310** arXiv:2604.18396 — "River-LLM: Large Language Model Seamless Exit Based on KV Share" — no — "KV Cache Absence" is a structural property of decoder-only attention (a skipped layer writes no K/V for later tokens), not a hardware or framework-version constraint. The authors' own evaluation is on a single A40, i.e. within the rig's hardware class, so this is not a hardware exclusion either. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18396))
- **C311** arXiv:2401.15024 — "SliceGPT: Compress Large Language Models by Deleting Rows and Columns" — yes — the negative RFT finding is scoped to the OPT family with the authors' own calibration data and is not a prediction for Qwen3-4B; the 25%-removal headline is a *width* result that the authors report as holding without RFT on Llama-2/OPT/Phi-2. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.15024))
- **C312** arXiv:2608.14003 — "Batch-wise Adaptive Pruning: Periodic Neuron Activation-Aware Weight Pruning for Language Reasoning Model" — no — the failure mode (one shared pruning mask per batch makes an offline-calibrated activation threshold wrong) is a property of batched serving, which is what the rig does. ([link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14003))

### C.2 Wontfix / not-planned / by-design  (0 items)

_This subsection found NOTHING, and that is a result, not an oversight._ **Neither vLLM nor SGLang has a `wontfix` or a `not planned` label at all** — the label queries return a literal "No results" in both repositories. Consequently there is no label-derived pool of explicit human refusals to draw on, and a bare `stateReason: not_planned` with zero human comments is not a closure reason under this map's rules, so such rows were dropped rather than counted. **Refusal-shaped reasons in this ecosystem live almost entirely in revert PRs**, which is why Section C is revert-weighted and why C.1/C.4 are large while C.2 is empty. Queries tried: `https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues?q=is%3Aissue+is%3Aclosed+label%3Awontfix` ; `https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues?q=label%3A%22not+planned%22` ; `https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues?q=label%3Awontfix` ; `https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/issues?q=label%3A%22not+planned%22` ; `is:issue "by design" vllm` ; `is:issue "won't fix" vllm` ; `is:issue "will not support" sglang`. Human refusal *sentences* that were found are recorded in C.1 with the PR or issue they were posted on.

| C203 | smb209 (author); closed unmerged with no maintainer comment; the same author notes the original report #37363 "closed as stale" | PR #53915 "[Bugfix] Fail loudly when a piecewise cudagraph piece is replayed with a changed input address"; related #37363 and #37361 | [1](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53915) ; [2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/37363) | "Piecewise pieces are replayed reading their inputs from the addresses recorded at capture time; CUDAGraphWrapper deliberately does not copy inputs (per its docstring). If a producer feeding a piece returns freshly-allocated outputs — e.g. an op added to splitting_ops that doesn't write into persistent buffers, such as vllm::moe_forward_shared (its outputs are fresh torch.empty_like tensors; it declares no mutates_args and no out-variant, unlike the attention ops which write persistent buffers) — those outputs land at new addresses every step, so the downstream piece replays reading stale memory and silently corrupts output." **REASON-MAY-HAVE-EXPIRED:** no — the `CUDAGraphWrapper` no-input-copy contract and the DEBUG-only address check are unchanged in 0.29.0. **S3b:** OCCUPIED (open PR #54851 "[Bugfix][Model] Bake real KV caches into captured fused_norm_rope under PIECEWISE (fixes silent garbage output for DSA models on multi-node TP)"; also open PR #53061 "[Bugfix][Compilation] Guard empty splitting ops before early return") | READ BODY |

The dominant fixed cost in #59681 was the response `Batcher` waiting out `MODEL_RESPONSE_BATCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 50 ms); the reporter measured that setting it to 0 drops TTFT p50 64.8 → 26.3 ms, and #64534 was opened to flush the first result immediately. The maintainer's remedy ("direct streaming") changes the recommended configuration, so the *default* numbers in these issues may no longer reproduce. The residual high-concurrency tail (TTFT 71 → 330 ms at c=32, 114 → 758 ms at c=64, throughput 5518 → 3634 tok/s) was explicitly flagged by the reporter as "architectural ... rather than a single fixable defect" and remained open at the time of the thread. **S3b:** SOLVED for the fixed-cost half (reporter-opened fix #64534 plus the maintainer-recommended direct-streaming configuration) / NO-ACTIVE-WORK-FOUND for the concurrency-scaling half (queries: `https://github.com/ray-project/ray/issues?q=is%3Aissue+is%3Aopen+serve+llm+streaming+overhead`; `https://github.com/ray-project/ray/issues?q=is%3Aissue+is%3Aopen+%22direct+streaming%22`) | READ BODY |

DoBi-SVD can fall back to a dense execution path for high-rank layers, allowing the backend to use an optimized dense GEMM instead of two smaller low-rank GEMMs. This hybrid runtime policy can improve latency, especially in decode-heavy settings, but it also means the measured speedup is not attributable to low-rank factorization alone." **REASON-MAY-HAVE-EXPIRED:** No expiry identified — the hybrid dense-fallback policy is a property of the compression method, not of a hardware generation or engine version. **S3b:** NO-ACTIVE-WORK-FOUND (queries: arXiv `searchtype=all&query=LLM+inference+acceleration`, `...efficient+LLM+serving`, `...quantization+end-to-end+serving+speedup`; GitHub `https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues?q=is%3Aissue+benchmark+batch+size+sweep+single` → 12 results, none an attribution-controlled speedup harness). As a result of this, a kernel that only computes (correctly) a portion of the output tensor would still pass the correctness check. We address this by running the tested kernel first and only after the reference implementation, thus avoiding this hack." "I I concluded that: - On GQA, the speed of flash-attention is basically close to flashinfer. - On MHA, the speed of flash-attention is significantly slower than flashinfer. As batch_size and kv_len increase, the difference gradually becomes more obvious, reaching a maximum gap of 3 times." **REASON-MAY-HAVE-EXPIRED:** Harness ordering only; the paper's own speedups are additionally scoped — "the speedups we measure in Section 3.2 are only accurate for those dimensions on our NVIDIA H200 GPUs" — and its headline is modest (mean speedup "from 0.53x to 1.10x of baseline (PyTorch Eager)"). The measurement is on H20 (a Hopper-class part) for MHA decode; the rig's Qwen3-4B is GQA, where the same report finds parity — hence held out of the count. **S3b:** NO-ACTIVE-WORK-FOUND for a vLLM/SGLang-side fix (queries: `https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues?q=KernelBench` → no hit); the fix lives in the authors' harness. NO-ACTIVE-WORK-FOUND for a fix (queries: `https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues?q="flash attention 3" sm120` → no sm120 FA3 enablement; vLLM's answer to this class is the FA version switch, A1.1). | READ BODY READ BODY |
